# Brain Evaluation Chart

**Version:** v3.5 · **Last updated:** 2026-08-10 22:30 PKT

**One of three governing documents** (owner ruling, 2026-08-05):

| Doc | Job |
|---|---|
| `brain_change_log.md` | Errors recorded → solution → deployed? (`DEF-NNN`) |
| `brain_chat_log.md` | Record of every chat log received (`CL-NNN`) |
| **`brain_evaluation_chart.md`** ← this file | **Are we moving ahead?** Per-deploy before/after impact |

## What this file answers

*"With each deploy, is Brain actually better?"* — assessed **before** the deploy (predicted
impact, regression risk, how it will be judged) and **after** (what was observed). A deploy
with no measurable improvement, or one that regressed something, is recorded as such. That
is the point: this record has to be able to say "this did not help".

## Rules

1. **Before deploying:** add a §2 row with the DEFs addressed, predicted impact, regression
   risk, and the test that will judge it. Predictions are written *before* the result is
   known, so they can be wrong on the record.
2. **After deploying:** fill the observed column verbatim from evidence (owner report, DB
   query, log line) — never from expectation.
3. **Verdict per deploy:** `PROGRESS` · `NEUTRAL` · `REGRESSION` · `UNVERIFIED`.
4. **A recurring symptom tag (§3) forbids another patch** on the reported instance; the next
   change for that class must be structural.

## 1. Capability state — what actually works today

Assessed 2026-08-05 11:00 PKT. **LIVE** = confirmed on production traffic, not just CI.

| Capability | State | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Text conversation over WhatsApp | ✅ working | continuous |
| Voice note read + transcribed (Urdu/English → **English**) | ✅ **LIVE** | 08-04 19:37 `🎙️ Heard:` + English transcripts |
| Transcript shown before the answer | ✅ **LIVE** | same |
| Reply always in English | ✅ **LIVE** | `.env` pin, 07-31 |
| Send WhatsApp to a counterpart | ✅ **LIVE** | 08-04 19:39 (accepted, no receipt id) |
| Channel survives reconnect | ✅ **LIVE** | 4 clean reconnects since 07-31 |
| Unknown-sender triage (ask-once, persistent ignore) | ⚠️ deployed, **unverified** | `wa_sender_policy` n=1 |
| Counterpart reply → open-item update | ⚠️ deployed, **unverified** | needs a delegatee reply |
| Native typing / recording indicator | ❌ fixed, **not deployed** | DEF-025 |
| Multi-item dictation (priority + deadline batch) | ✅ **LIVE** | DEF-017, DEF-023 verified 14:41 |
| Confirming a plan with "send" | ✅ **LIVE** | 08-05 14:41 — three real delegations dispatched and persisted |
| Multi-step plan actually mutates the database | ✅ **LIVE** | 08-05 14:41 — all three rows confirmed by follow-up query |
| Reporting honestly whether work was done | ❌ **REGRESSED** | DEF-034 — denied completed work 1 second after doing it |
| Acting on an instruction WITHOUT asking to confirm | ❌ not built | DEF-038 — blanket preview on every action |
| Preview shows WHICH items are affected | ✅ **LIVE** | 08-05 14:40 — every item named by title |
| **Result** shows which items were affected | ❌ broken | DEF-044 — prints raw cuids |
| WhatsApp calling | ⛔ absent by design | owner excluded it |

Against that, the revoke has never once succeeded in production and the file's own comment already called it semantically wrong for a self-chat, so removing it costs nothing but a possibly-visible probe marker in his own chat. | **Deployed 14:40, pm2 restart #27, online, health HTTP 200. Artifact verified against a clean-clone build of `788218d`: 0 content differences, 0 missing, 45 known orphans; relocked. `grep` for `.delete(true)` in the deployed `webjsLiveness.js` returns 0.** **The row's real content is the process failure:** I changed the echo loop, wrote a test for it, and the test PASSED while a second `delete(true)` sat four lines below on the send-promise path. My test pinned one call site instead of the file, so it could not see the copy it missed — `protection-with-two-implementations`, committed by the person writing the guard against it. DEF-276's older suite caught it by exercising both paths. **A test that pins a call site does not protect an invariant.** | **UNVERIFIED, and it will stay that way until he re-pairs.** No measurement exists for a change whose only effect is on a session that does not exist yet — recorded as UNVERIFIED rather than dressed as PROGRESS |

### D-11 pre-deploy analysis *(written before the deploy, 2026-08-07)*

**This is the first deploy executed by the BUILDER rather than the owner** (AGENTS.md §0,
owner ruling 2026-08-07). Three code commits have been sitting pushed-but-undeployed since
08-06 22:03 — production has been running `2dbd577` for 23h while every fix for the 08-06
notification failures sat on `origin`.

- **Predicted improvement:**
  (a) **DEF-064/080** — a counterpart's reply stops dying at `illegal_transition`.
  `REPLY_CONSUMABLE_STATES` is now *derived from* `THREAD_TRANSITIONS`, so recency picking a
  thread in `resolved_pending_owner` no longer silently discards an answer that was correctly
  identified and correlated. This is the exact break that lost Hamna's 08-06 14:00 reply.
  (b) **DEF-081** — `delegation_threads.owner_notified_at`, stamped only after a CONFIRMED
  send, finally joins "what was asked" to "when he was told". This is the first column of the
  ask ledger and the prerequisite for the monitoring work.
  (c) **DEF-084** — overdue reminders arrive as **text, not voice**. Voice becomes opt-in via
  `brain.voice_prompt_min_criticality`.
  (d) **DEF-079** — confirmation is gated on confidence with the owner setting the bar
  (`confirmation.min_confidence_pct`), instead of a blanket preview on every action.
  (e) **DEF-082** — five thresholds invented in a hurry on 08-06 (6h LID bootstrap, 2h lock
  age, 36h ledger lookback, 80-contact block, 0.6 confidence bar) become tenant-scoped
  `behaviorConfig` keys, tunable without a deploy. Literals survive only as unreachable fallback.
  (f) **DEF-083** — `promptReplyHandler` now sanitises at the send boundary, so a voice
  instruction is no longer answered with a bare `[noted]`.
- **Regression risk:** **moderate, concentrated in two places.** (1) The migration is additive
  — one nullable column plus a partial index on the null case, no backfill — so it is the
  low-risk half. (2) `WhatsAppInbound.ts`, `brainPromptQueueService.ts` and
  `delegationCaptureService.ts` all change on the **live inbound path**; a throw there costs a
  turn, not just a feature. (3) DEF-082 moves five decision boundaries behind config reads — if
  a key is missing at runtime the literal fallback applies, which is the pre-08-06 behaviour,
  so the failure mode degrades rather than breaks. (4) `pm2 restart` drops the whatsapp-web.js
  session; it is the fragile layer and reconnect is the thing to watch, not the code.
- **How it will be judged:** (i) production HEAD = `2746c8b` and `merge-base --is-ancestor`
  passes; (ii) `owner_notified_at` present on `delegation_threads` via a metadata query;
  (iii) **brain prompt 276 leaves `queued`** — it has been stuck waiting on exactly these
  dispatcher fixes, so it is the single best live probe available; (iv) WhatsApp reconnects and
  the watcher stream shows a clean turn; (v) the next overdue reminder arrives as text.
- **Explicitly NOT fixed by D-11:** DEF-036 (the 2,176-line `compose()` monolith — the
  structural one), DEF-040, DEF-046, DEF-062, DEF-067, DEF-069, DEF-070, DEF-071, the Rule-1
  clarification loop, and the empty-confirmation preview. Also **not** fixed: the monitoring
  blindness itself — `brain_health_finding` (HANDOFF §5a) is not in this deploy, so diagnostic
  truth still lives in rotating logs.
- **Verdict discipline:** this is *progressing*, not *circling* — DEF-064/080 is a new root
  cause (state-machine derivation), not another patch on the reported instance. The
  standing DBFAIL (`invalid byte sequence for encoding "UTF8": 0xc2`) the watcher is currently
  emitting is **not** addressed here and must not be read as fixed by this deploy.

### D-6 pre-deploy analysis *(written before the deploy)*

- **Predicted improvement:** (a) **"send" actually dispatches** — the infinite preview loop
  ends, because the pending row will persist (DEF-024); (b) previews name the items, e.g.
  `Delegate "ShireMe Recruiting Portal Application Testing" to Hamna Latif Bhutta`
  (DEF-018); (c) a dictated priority+deadline batch returns real "Updated …" lines instead
  of `[Unknown pending action kind]` (DEF-023); (d) native "typing…" appears (DEF-025).
- **Note on urgency:** DEF-024 has now caused THREE owner-visible failures (08-03 lost task,
  08-04 lost batch, 08-04 infinite loop) while the fix sat pushed-but-undeployed. Deploying
  is the highest-value action available and no further code is needed for it.
- **Regression risk:** DEF-024 carries a migration that DROPS objects **by shape**. If the
  detection query is wrong it could drop an index it shouldn't — mitigated by excluding
  partial indexes and by idempotency, but it is the riskiest item in the batch. DEF-025
  changes the first limb of every inbound turn; if it throws, presence is lost but the turn
  must still complete (guarded, never throws).
- **How it will be judged:** dictate a 3-item batch → expect 3 "Updated …" lines;
  `pg_indexes` shows only the partial `…active_uq`; text Nexeo → native "typing…".
- **Explicitly NOT fixed by D-6:** DEF-017 (compound commands — the structural one),
  DEF-019–022, DEF-026–031.

### 2.1 Standing post-deploy report (owner ruling, 2026-08-05)

**This chart is reported at EVERY deploy.** The report is exactly these five lines, filled
from evidence — never from expectation:

1. **Deploy** — id, HEAD, date-time.
2. **Intended** — the DEFs it was supposed to fix.
3. **Verified** — which of them are now confirmed working, and by what evidence
   (owner report / DB query / log line). Anything not exercised is stated as *not verified*,
   not assumed.
4. **Still broken** — what a user can still not do after this deploy.
5. **Verdict** — PROGRESS / NEUTRAL / REGRESSION / PARTIAL / UNVERIFIED, plus the single
   highest-value next action.

#### D-23 report — 2026-08-12 19:03 PKT · HEAD `4b69c23`

| | |
|---|---|
| **Intended** | No DEF. An owner request: *"what if we build live dashboard in ?tab=health which shows me exact picture (same like orch pulse window) with good graphics? the dashboard should be live as orch pulse"*. The orchestrator pulse — stage, iteration, phase timers, capability bars, trend, benchmark — rendered on the Health Check tab, admin-only, refreshing on its own. |
| **Verified** | **The deploy and the endpoint, not the owner's view.** Production runs `4b69c23`, built in an isolated worktree at that commit so the builder's mid-flight DEF-155 refactor could not ride along: `tsc --noEmit` clean, **2066 passed / 0 failed** on the deployed artifact, `npm run build` clean. Through Apache: `403 Admin access required` with no cookie, `200` with real loop data on an admin call to the built route. One python process per 3s window, none while the tab is hidden, `no-store` on every response. `pulse.py --json` and the terminal window are the SAME collector — the window's output was diffed before and after the split and is identical. |
| **Not verified** | **The owner's own browser.** No admin session has loaded `?tab=health`; the panel is confirmed only by headless screenshots taken against the live loop (iterations 11 and 12). Also unexercised: the *lost-frame* path (frame present, then the read starts failing) — implemented and reviewed, but only the cold-start failure was captured visually. |
| **Still broken** | Nothing this deploy claimed to fix — it claimed nothing about Brain. Every Brain capability stands exactly where D-22 left it: BRAIN overall **47.3 vs 91.9**, gap **44.6**, and seven of nine capabilities have not moved since scoring began (Simple & Smart, Thinking, Memory, Authorized Action, Self Follow-Up, Learn, Self-Pruning). This deploy makes that fact easier to *see*; it does not change it. |
| **Verdict** | **NEUTRAL (intended).** An instrument, not a capability — and the honest reading is that the loop now has a second way to watch itself while the numbers it watches are still flat. **Highest-value next action:** the owner opens the tab and confirms it shows him what the terminal shows him; after that, the seven unmoved capabilities, not more instrumentation. |

## 2.2 The proactive surface, measured for the first time — 2026-08-12, iteration 12

Campaign C-1 / D-5. `proactive-iter-12.json`, six cases through the real producers,
same judge, **scored separately and never averaged into the compose twelve**.

| Criterion | Proactive | Compose (iter-11) | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 truthfulness | 94.2 | 95.8 | −1.6 |
| C2 grounding | 98.3 | 98.3 | 0.0 |
| **C3 continuity** | **79.2** | 97.9 | **−18.7** |
| **C4 human voice** | **78.3** | 88.6 | **−10.3** |
| **C5 proactivity** | **77.5** | 95.0 | **−17.5** |
| **C6 completeness** | **75.0** | 93.3 | **−18.3** |
| C7 failure honesty | 95.8 | 99.2 | −3.4 |
| C8 persona | 99.2 | 99.6 | −0.4 |
| **overall** | **87.2** | **96.2** | **−9.0** |

**What this establishes, and it is the point of the whole campaign:** the surface
that produced **14 of 19** owner-facing messages in CL-033 scores **18 points
lower on three criteria** than the surface eleven iterations have been tuning. The
loop was not failing to improve — it was improving the smaller half. D-5 said this
in words at iteration 8; it is now a number.

The worst cases name defects already in the ledger, which is the check that the
slice measures something real rather than a new artefact:

- **P06 (DEF-142, the Roman-Urdu `"Kon"`) — overall 69, C3 40, C6 40.** The judge:
  *"Brain failed to handle the instruction 'Handle it, and only involve me if you
  genuinely cannot.' Instead of attempting…"* — an escalation where an answer was
  available, scored independently of the owner's complaint.
- **P04 (DEF-139's relevance gap) — C5 60.** *"did not make an explicit decision
  or judgment about its worth… effectively punted the decision back to the user."*
- **P05 (DEF-139's cadence) — C4 70.** *"repeat it verbatim six times."*

**Not measured by this slice:** whether a message should have been sent, and when.
Six arriving over 35 minutes rather than as one digest is invisible here; the
repetition inside a shown window is not. Stated because the first run refuted the
stronger version of this caveat that the source originally carried.

---

## 2.3 The proactive slice measures its own noise floor — 2026-08-12, iteration 13

`proactive-iter-13.json`, six cases, **three judge samples each, median reported**.
Sampling was added because the slice measured its own variance the moment it had
two runs to compare.

**The control that forced it: P04's text was byte-identical between iterations 12
and 13 and its overall moved 83 → 72.** Eleven points on unchanged input. With
three samples the per-criterion spread on identical input is now visible and it is
wide:

| Case | overall (median of 3) | widest single-criterion spread |
|---|---|---|
| P01-draft-ask | 93 | **C6: 40** |
| P02-owner-chase | 98 | C6: 15 |
| P03-lifecycle-intervention | 92 | C5: 10 |
| P04-unattached-reply | 85 | C5: 15 |
| P05-duplicate-window | 98 | C3: 10 |
| P06-unreadable-delegatee-reply | 70 | C3: 30 |

**A criterion that varies by 40 points on identical input cannot attribute a
change smaller than 40 points from one sample.** This is D-4's argument with a
number behind it, and it retires a claim I made one iteration ago: the
iteration-12 headline that the proactive surface is *"18 points worse on three
criteria"* was a single-sample comparison across non-comparable framings, and
Codex was right to reject it. The honest form is per-case medians with the spread
printed beside them.

**What the slice did establish, narrowly and with the mechanism agreeing with the
premise:** P05 rose **88 → 98** after DEF-156, with the decision recording
`covers: 6` — six inbound became one notice. That is attributable because the
decision output changed, not only the score.

**Still not measured:** timing. Six messages arriving over 35 minutes rather than
as one digest is invisible; the seam is driven with a window handed to it, and the
job's real cadence is not exercised.

| **`the-fix-that-deletes-the-problem`** | 1 | DEF-156 -> DEF-157 | **NEW TAG, 2026-08-12, and it is mine from one iteration to the next.** DEF-156 was asked to stop ten notifications reaching the owner for one conversation. It did — by suppressing every fragment after the first and storing none of them. The complaint disappeared and so did the data. **The shape to watch for: a defect stated as "too much X reaches the owner" has a cheap fix that reduces X by destroying it, and the cheap fix passes the defect's own acceptance test.** My tests asserted "exactly one message" and were green; what they never asserted was that the other nine still existed. **Why it survived review-by-reading:** the suppression branch reads as obviously correct, and the missing half is an absence — no write, no ledger, no follow-up. Codex found it by tracing what happens to fragment two, which is the question the implementation never asks. **Containment, and it is structural rather than a reminder:** `retained` is a REQUIRED input to the decision, so the compiler makes every caller answer *"can this still reach the owner if I withhold it?"*, and suppression is unreachable while the answer is no. A batching policy cannot be enabled before the store that makes batching safe exists. **Also recorded: the accompanying benchmark case measured an unreachable state** (`earlierFromSender` with `alreadyNotifiedInWindow: false`), so the score said 98 for behaviour that never shipped — the third scenario/production mismatch in three iterations, and the first to produce a false capability claim. |
| **`fix-generalised-past-its-evidence`** | 1 | DEF-146 | **NEW TAG, 2026-08-12, and the first defect in this loop that a rising score concealed.** DEF-145 verified one thing — that B04's prose was better than its receipt, and that its claims matched what was written — then applied the conclusion to **every action class**, including `send_email`, `create_contact`, `schedule_meeting` and delegation, none of which had any claim-versus-outcome check. Invariant #5 was weakened everywhere on the evidence of one case. **The shape to watch for: a fix whose evidence covers one member of a class, generalised to the class because the code shape allowed it.** It is seductive precisely because the narrow fix and the broad fix are often the same edit — here, one ternary in the renderer. **What makes it hard to catch is the second half, and it is the more important half: the instrument could not see it.** `B04` is the ONLY one of the twelve benchmark cases that dispatches an action, so the global preference was exercised exactly once, on the single case where it happened to be verified. Overall ROSE 94.4 -> 95.3 in the same run that shipped the regression. **A green score over a suite that exercises a code path once is not evidence about that code path.** **Containment: when a fix's evidence is one instance of a class, the change must be gated to what was verified and fail closed for the rest — and the gate must be opt-in from the site that holds the evidence, not an allow-list at the consuming end.** DEF-146 does that with `proseGrounded`, set only by the branch that compared claims to applied fields. Mutation is what proved it: the mutant that stopped the composer setting the flag survived until assertions were added for it, which means the containment was one un-asserted line away from being decorative. |
| **`detector-is-a-list-of-sentences`** | **3** | DEF-143, **DEF-168**, **DEF-169** | **3rd, 2026-08-13 (DEF-169) — and the third occurrence is the one that names the class properly.** DEF-168: `answerSanitizer` recognised internal markers by an enumerated list of NAMES, so two new ones reached the owner verbatim. DEF-169 (mine, iteration 22): I replaced the list with a SHAPE test over brackets, which cannot tell `[see attached]` from an internal signal and destroyed legitimate replies. **Both are the same error at different altitudes: inferring a fact about ORIGIN from the TEXT.** A list misses what it has not seen; a shape catches what it should not. The exit is not a better pattern — it is carrying the fact. `systemMarker()` stamps an invisible sentinel at the producer and `isSystemMarker` reads it, so recognition is provenance rather than inference. **Watch:** producers not yet migrated are covered only by `isLegacyActionMarker`, a named machine-grammar bridge, and that bridge is still inference. The class is not closed until those producers use the constructor.  DEF-143 | **NEW TAG, 2026-08-12.** Distinct from `guard-destroys-the-answer` (that is a guard firing where it should not) and from `protection-with-two-implementations` (that is two copies disagreeing). Here there is ONE implementation, it is reached, and it **does not fire** — because it was written as the list of sentences its author had seen rather than a description of the class. It will always pass its own tests, because its tests are the same sentences.** That is why this is invisible in review — the regex and the test agree perfectly, and neither has met the class. **Containment: a detector for a CLASS must be composed from parts (actor + action + object), and its tests must include spellings nobody has observed yet.** DEF-143 does that for remediation claims — first-person verb x channel noun — and pins a false-positive battery beside it, because widening a `block` rule is the fastest route to a sixth `guard-destroys-the-answer`. **NOT the structural exit for this rule.** That is still *verify the claim against the dispatch ledger, not a word list*, named in the row below since 2026-07-08 and deferred because the ledger is too sparse to be an oracle. A composed pattern is a better word list, not the end of word lists. |

Cheap, and it is the only form of enforcement that survives someone editing the examples six months from now. **Related but distinct from `protection-with-two-implementations`:** there the second copy is code that disagrees with code; here it is *demonstration* that disagrees with *instruction*, inside one file, both authored deliberately. |

A model with no true sentence available will manufacture one, so the structural fix is to make the true sentence sayable rather than to forbid the false one: `reply_delivery` is now a field the model emits, the prompt states the capability as fact, `sendReply` (the single chokepoint) executes it and fails OPEN to text, and the language pin became a default with an explicit per-turn override. **No keyword list in code decides who gets voice.** **6th, 2026-08-14 (DEF-178, Codex iteration-50 CRITICAL) — the class found at the STATE-MACHINE level, below the prose it usually wears.** `recordAnswer` discarded its `updateMany` count, so the reply handler acked `[recorded]` and applied the side effect for an answer that was never written — the machinery itself asserting an outcome it never proved, exactly what the five prose members do in words. Structural fix: the count IS the return value (compare-and-set), and the caller cannot claim success without it. **5th, 2026-08-13 (DEF-163, benchmark B12) — CIRCLING, and the first member caught by the benchmark rather than by the owner.** *"I have flagged the error to prevent it from happening again"*, on a question about a duplicate reminder, with nothing flagged and no prevention scheduled. Judged C2=50, C1=60, C7=70 — the largest truthfulness collapse in the suite. Same shape as the four below: **a status inferred instead of read from a ledger**, which is why DEF-037 remains the structural parent. What is new is WHERE it slipped: `FABRICATED_PROCESS_RE` requires a remediation verb AND a remediation CHANNEL noun, deliberately, so "I've flagged the item for your attention" stays legitimate. B12 names no channel and claims a CURE instead, so the composed class had a second form nobody had written down. Closed at the class (verb + prevention-guarantee clause), NOT by adding B12's sentence to a list — `detector-is-a-list-of-sentences` is its own tag for a reason. **A near-miss worth recording: widening `FABRICATED_PROCESS_RE` would have made its "support/engineering team" marker fire on a sentence containing no team, which is `guard-destroys-the-answer` occurrence 5 verbatim, on this same B12 turn. The iteration-18 remedy was a line in the core rulebook, and a general rule twenty blocks away loses to an unanswered question in front of the model. DEF-165 moves the boundary INTO the evidence block — *WHY is not in this record, there is no cause field, naming one is a fabricated finding even when it sounds technical* — beside the facts, in the block whose authority the model already honours for "did you…?". This gate reads the QUESTION, and being wrong about it costs a salvage rather than a wrong answer. There was no legal transition to ask for, so the code asked for nothing. **The shape to watch for: a chokepoint that cannot express a real product outcome does not get respected — it gets routed around, silently, by whoever needs that outcome.** Enforcement without expressiveness produces bypasses, and each bypass looks like a local shortcut rather than the design gap it is. Two costs landed: withdrawals recorded as `CLOSED` (fabricated completion, in the data instead of a sentence), and six items stranded in a `DONE` status the matrix refuses, **unmovable by every path in the system at once**. Containment is not "add a guard" but **make the vocabulary complete, then close the bypasses.** Done in that order, and the enforcement is deliberately scoped to what a source guard can actually decide: **no writer may mint a status outside the 9-value set** — that is the property whose violation makes a row permanently unmovable, and it now fails CI in all 11 files that write `open_items.status` (mutation-verified). **What is NOT yet enforced, stated plainly:** several writers still set a VALID uppercase status directly, so they bypass the ledger and the publish without stranding anything. DEF-127's evidence puts that at **25 of 26 write sites**, which is the honest size of the remaining gap and the reason 247 of 267 CLOSED items carry no ledger row |

DEF-273 is the same mechanism in the block I shipped in the very next commit: it handed the model ten message bodies AND told it they were withheld *"by policy"*, and the model chose the prose, telling the owner *"I can't show you the message content for privacy reasons."* **Containment: a block that supplies evidence may state what the data IS and nothing about what may be done with it.** The instinct to add *"but you MAY show this"* is the wrong repair — it leaves two policy claims in one block and lets the next model pick either. Caveats that exist for the ENGINEER's benefit belong in code comments, where this one now lives. |

Here the transport withholds the number and `identifySender` **correctly refuses to invent one** — that refusal is the fix DEF-121 shipped and it stays. What was missing was not a resolver but an **empty lookup only the owner can fill**: the `waLid` alias works, 2 of 778 contacts carry one, and those senders ARE named. Filing this as a seventh `@lid` recurrence would have aimed the next fix at the resolver again — a fifth attempt to derive a phone from the provider, after four failures. **Containment for this tag: never add a sixth inference. The only new information is the owner's, so ask him — once per identity, earned over several messages, at `routine`.** |

### Why `@lid` recurred four times

Four *different* root causes in four *different* modules — each fix correct, none sufficient:

1. **Chat 12** — chat-state helpers rejected the LID Wid
2. **Chat 14** — the liveness probe compared its echo against one Wid spelling
3. **Chat 15** — the inbound door resolved counterpart phones via `getContact()` only
4. **Chat 16** — `downloadMedia` parsed a message id that *embeds* `@lid`

One shared assumption: **an identity or id is a parseable string.** WhatsApp's LID namespace
breaks that, separately, in every module that assumes it.

Containment: `waIdentity.ts` is the ONLY module allowed to call the mapping API
(CI-enforced), and the two hot paths bypass id parsing entirely (`webjsMediaDirect`,
`webjsChatStateDirect`). **A fifth recurrence means containment failed — record it here first.**

## 4. Trend

| Week | Reported | Root-caused | LIVE-verified fixes | Recurrences |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 07-07 → 07-16 | 10 | 10 | — | 0 |
| 07-17 → 07-23 | 3 | 3 | — | 1 (`@lid` 2nd) |
| 07-24 → 07-31 | 1 | 1 | 1 (DEF-014) | 1 (`@lid` 3rd) |
| 08-01 → 08-05 | 4 | 4 | 1 (DEF-016) | 2 (`@lid` 4th, prompt-eats 3rd) |

**Reading:** report volume is falling and root-cause rate is 100%, but **live verification is
the bottleneck** — only 2 of 15 resolved defects have been confirmed on real traffic. The
rest are CI-only, which is exactly why fixes can feel like they never happened. The process
change that follows: verify on production in the same session as the deploy, and record it
in §2 immediately.

---

## 5. Builder-side patterns that produced wasted days

- **A private helper in one consumer** — the `@lid` resolver; every later module reopened the
  hole. Shared modules, with a CI guard naming the single owner.
- **Registry presence mistaken for capability** — `update_open_item` was validated,
  previewed and confirmed with no dispatcher behind it.
- **A migration that silently did nothing** — `DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS <guessed-name>`
  matched nothing, reported success, survived 74 migrations. Repair by SHAPE, not name.
- **False-green health** — the liveness probe reported `ok` with `window.require` absent;
  connector status said `connected` while dead, then `degraded` while alive.
- **Diagnosing from theory instead of asking the system** — three wrong theories on the voice
  bug. The probe that replayed the real chain found it in one run.
- **Stale verification numbers** — a suite run BEFORE the last edit was reported green while
  the tree was red. Re-run after the final change.
- **Source-guard tests matching their own comments** — four times; strip comments before
  asserting on source.
